Um… I guess I’m here because my eating has been feeling a little out of control. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but what scares me is that once I start eating—especially if I’m alone in the kitchen—it’s like I sort of step aside and can’t make myself stop. Sorry—am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you have times where you eat a large amount of food in a short period, like I've described, without doing anything afterwards to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is what happens. I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. But then it turns into a full plate, and then a couple slices of banana bread, and chips, and cheese, and whatever cookies are in the tin. And I’ll realize after that it was… a lot. More than I would ever serve someone else as one meal, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I had to sort of look back at my kitchen calendar, because I didn’t want to exaggerate. But it looks like it’s been happening most weeks… usually once, sometimes twice in a week, especially around the food bank weeks.\n\nAnd that’s been going on for many months now, I think. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.

But the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.

I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. I wish I could say it’s only when I’m upset or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m tense or having a hard day, but I don’t think I’ve paid close enough attention to say it can’t happen at other times. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I can’t honestly say it only happens when I’m upset.\n\nWhat scares me is that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it’s like I’ve stepped aside somehow. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you lost control while eating and you were feeling happy or neutral?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a clear example, and I’m sorry, it’s a little hard to separate out my mood at the start.\n\nBut yes, I think there have been times where I wasn’t especially upset. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because my eating has been feeling a little out of control. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but what scares me is that once I start eating—especially if I’m alone in the kitchen—it’s like I sort of step aside and can’t make myself stop. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away,” and I just… don’t. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating, do you ever do anything to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I do anything like… purposely to cancel it out, if that’s what you mean. I might feel guilty and tell myself I’ll be “better” the next day, but I don’t know if that counts. Sorry—am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you have times where you eat a large amount of food in a short period, like I've described, without doing anything afterwards to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is what happens. I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. But then it turns into a full plate, and then a couple slices of banana bread, and chips, and cheese, and whatever cookies are in the tin. And I’ll realize after that it was… a lot. More than I would ever serve someone else as one meal, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I had to sort of look back at my kitchen calendar, because I didn’t want to exaggerate. But it looks like it’s been happening most weeks… usually once, sometimes twice in a week, especially around the food bank weeks.\n\nAnd that’s been going on for many months now, I think. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. I wish I could say it’s only when I’m upset or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m tense or having a hard day, but I don’t think I’ve paid close enough attention to say it can’t happen at other times. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I can’t honestly say it only happens when I’m upset.\n\nWhat scares me is that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it’s like I’ve stepped aside somehow. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you lost control while eating and you were feeling happy or neutral?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a clear example, and I’m sorry, it’s a little hard to separate out my mood at the start.\n\nBut yes, I think there have been times where I wasn’t especially upset. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore. I’d be telling myself, Janet, put it away, stop now, and I just… didn’t.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way, but that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t weigh myself very often, partly because I don’t like seeing the number.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s a significant gain or loss. I don’t think there’s been anything dramatic that I’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yes… I think so. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but I don’t think it only happens when I’m emotional.

Sometimes I can start out feeling pretty neutral, just in the kitchen by myself, and then once I start eating it’s like I sort of… step aside. I’m still thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” but I can’t quite make myself do it.

I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away,” and I just… don’t. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating, do you ever do anything to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I do anything like… purposely to cancel it out, if that’s what you mean. I might feel guilty and tell myself I’ll be “better” the next day, but I don’t know if that counts. Sorry—am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you have times where you eat a large amount of food in a short period, like I've described, without doing anything afterwards to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is what happens. I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. But then it turns into a full plate, and then a couple slices of banana bread, and chips, and cheese, and whatever cookies are in the tin. And I’ll realize after that it was… a lot. More than I would ever serve someone else as one meal, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I had to sort of look back at my kitchen calendar, because I didn’t want to exaggerate. But it looks like it’s been happening most weeks… usually once, sometimes twice in a week, especially around the food bank weeks.\n\nAnd that’s been going on for many months now, I think. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore.     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because my eating has been feeling a little out of control. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but what scares me is that once I start eating—especially if I’m alone in the kitchen—it’s like I sort of step aside and can’t make myself stop. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away,” and I just… don’t. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating, do you ever do anything to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think I do anything like… purposely to cancel it out, if that’s what you mean. I might feel guilty and tell myself I’ll be “better” the next day, but I don’t know if that counts. Sorry—am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you have times where you eat a large amount of food in a short period, like I've described, without doing anything afterwards to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is what happens. I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. But then it turns into a full plate, and then a couple slices of banana bread, and chips, and cheese, and whatever cookies are in the tin. And I’ll realize after that it was… a lot. More than I would ever serve someone else as one meal, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I had to sort of look back at my kitchen calendar, because I didn’t want to exaggerate. But it looks like it’s been happening most weeks… usually once, sometimes twice in a week, especially around the food bank weeks.\n\nAnd that’s been going on for many months now, I think. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. I wish I could say it’s only when I’m upset or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m tense or having a hard day, but I don’t think I’ve paid close enough attention to say it can’t happen at other times. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I can’t honestly say it only happens when I’m upset.\n\nWhat scares me is that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it’s like I’ve stepped aside somehow. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you lost control while eating and you were feeling happy or neutral?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a clear example, and I’m sorry, it’s a little hard to separate out my mood at the start.\n\nBut yes, I think there have been times where I wasn’t especially upset. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore. Sorry—am I answering that the right way?\n\nDoctor: Do you have times where you eat a large amount of food in a short period, like I've described, without doing anything afterwards to try to undo the effects of what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is what happens. I don’t do anything afterward to, um, undo it.\n\nIt’s usually not every day, but some afternoons—like after I’ve been at the food bank, or if I’ve been baking for something at the senior center—I’ll come home and it starts like I’m just having leftovers. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you lost control while eating and you were feeling happy or neutral?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a clear example, and I’m sorry, it’s a little hard to separate out my mood at the start.\n\nBut yes, I think there have been times where I wasn’t especially upset. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore. More than I would ever serve someone else as one meal, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I had to sort of look back at my kitchen calendar, because I didn’t want to exaggerate. But it looks like it’s been happening most weeks… usually once, sometimes twice in a week, especially around the food bank weeks.\n\nAnd that’s been going on for many months now, I think. I know that probably sounds bad… I just want to be accurate.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I mean, I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know people have real problems, but afterward I feel really awful about myself.\n\nI’ll sit there thinking, why did I do that, and I’ll feel ashamed—like I wasted food I meant to share, or that I’m too old to be acting this way. And then I usually promise myself it won’t happen again. It’s embarrassing to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not completely sure. I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, honestly.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s a real change or just me being self-conscious. I wouldn’t want to say it’s significant if I’m not sure. Should I have been keeping track?\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of eating a large amount of food, do you feel like you're unable to control what or how much you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s the part that scares me the most, honestly.\n\nOnce I start, especially if I’m by myself in the kitchen, it’s like I’m not fully in charge anymore. I’ll be telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I can hear that in my head, but I still keep going. I don’t know if that makes sense… I’m not trying to be dramatic, but it really does feel like I can’t make myself stop in that moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period, without being able to stop, happen even when you're not feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I think I notice it more when I’ve had a hard day or I’m upset, but… I don’t know that I’ve really paid attention to whether it happens when everything is fine.\n\nI don’t want to give you the wrong answer. It could be that there are times when I’m not obviously stressed, but I’ve just sort of gotten used to not noticing what I’m feeling. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed or emotional right before these episodes of eating a large amount of food?\n\nPatient: I think… probably a lot of the time, yes, but I’m not totally sure how to separate it out.\n\nI don’t always stop and think, “I’m stressed right now,” before it happens. Sometimes I only realize afterward that I was upset or wound up. I don’t want to overstate it, because maybe there are times it happens and I wasn’t feeling anything obvious.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your emotions, such as feeling stressed or upset, play a role in triggering these episodes where you lose control while eating?\n\nPatient: I think they might, yes… but I’m a little hesitant to say that like it’s always the reason.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m upset or tense, or after I’ve been holding things in. But sometimes I don’t really know what I was feeling until after it’s already happened. So… I guess emotions probably do play a part, I’m just not sure how much. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there specific types of food that you tend to lose control with during these episodes, or can it happen with any type of food?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s one specific food, exactly… I wish I could give you a cleaner answer.\n\nIt’s more like, once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, something kind of takes over. I’ll be telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… don’t. That’s the part that scares me, more than the particular food.\n\nDoes that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating in the same way if you were with other people?\n\nPatient: Probably not in the same way… or at least, I don’t think so.\n\nI’m embarrassed to say it, but it’s more when I’m alone, like in the kitchen. That’s when it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside and can’t get myself to stop, even while I’m thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now.” If someone else were there, I think I’d be more aware of myself and maybe able to keep it together better.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would lose control while eating when you're feeling happy or neutral, or does it only happen when you're feeling stressed, upset, or other negative emotions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s only when I’m upset… I mean, that’s when I notice it more, probably.\n\nBut the part that scares me is less the exact mood I’m in and more that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it feels like I’ve sort of stepped aside. I’m telling myself the whole time, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just… can’t seem to make myself do it.\n\nSo I don’t want to say it never happens if I’m feeling okay, because I’m not sure that would be honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a large amount of food usually happen when you're feeling upset or stressed, or can they happen at any time regardless of your emotional state?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not completely sure. I wish I could say it’s only when I’m upset or stressed, because that would make more sense, I guess.\n\nI do notice it more when I’m tense or having a hard day, but I don’t think I’ve paid close enough attention to say it can’t happen at other times. Sometimes it feels like it’s less about the emotion and more about being alone and starting to eat, and then I don’t stop the way I mean to.\n\nIs that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling calm and relaxed?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe, yes. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I can’t honestly say it only happens when I’m upset.\n\nWhat scares me is that once I start eating, especially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it’s like I’ve stepped aside somehow. I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” and I just don’t seem able to make myself stop.\n\nSo even if I started out calm, I think that could still happen. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times when you lost control while eating and you were feeling happy or neutral?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a clear example, and I’m sorry, it’s a little hard to separate out my mood at the start.\n\nBut yes, I think there have been times where I wasn’t especially upset. I might have just been in the kitchen by myself, and once I started eating, it was like I wasn’t really in charge anymore. I’d be telling myself, Janet, put it away, stop now, and I just… didn’t.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way, but that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight gain or loss over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t weigh myself very often, partly because I don’t like seeing the number.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s a significant gain or loss. I don’t think there’s been anything dramatic that I’ve noticed. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever lose control while eating when you're feeling neither happy, sad, nor stressed, just a completely neutral state?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to exaggerate it, but I don’t think it only happens when I’m emotional.\n\nSometimes I can start out feeling pretty neutral, just in the kitchen by myself, and then once I start eating it’s like I sort of… step aside. I’m still thinking, “Janet, put it away, stop now,” but I can’t quite make myself do it.\n\nThat sounds bad when I say it out loud, but yes, I think it can happen even if I wasn’t happy or sad or stressed at the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Is there a particular emotion that you always or almost always feel right before losing control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s one emotion that’s always there, no.\n\nI wish I could give you a clearer answer. Sometimes I notice I’m tense or embarrassed afterward, but right before… I’m not sure it’s that consistent. It can be kind of ordinary at first, if that makes sense. I don’t want to say the wrong thing, but I haven’t noticed one specific feeling that always comes before it.\n\nDoctor: Is losing control while eating always associated with some kind of emotional state for you?\n\nPatient: Not always, I don’t think. I mean… sometimes there is a feeling there, but the part that scares me is more what happens once I’ve started.\n\nEspecially if I’m alone in the kitchen, it can feel like I’ve sort of stepped aside, and I’m telling myself, “Janet, put it away, stop,” but I just… don’t. So I wouldn’t want to say it’s always tied to an emotion beforehand. Is that answering it the right way?"
